# Rust rewrite compatibility contracts and parity acceptance matrix

This document freezes the externally observable behavior of p-track's Go
implementation for Plan 8, the Rust/Tauri rewrite.

The sole compatibility baseline is annotated tag `v0.21.0`, commit
`b7727c5ef0e6a924ff43e0eafdeb7ad4f7c1e883`, released 2026-08-11. Four
commits landed after that tag on the branch used for inventory extraction.
A tag-to-branch diff audit removed or rewrote every affected contract. The
later Help Center, expanded native menus, close/runtime-call fencing,
overlay-aware renderer recovery, and accessibility/layout work are not
`v0.21.0` behavior. The tagged baseline remains identified as such, and
GUI-128–138 plus TERM-107–108 deliberately catalog those current-feature
extensions required for final cutover.

## Scope and method

Parity means preserving what another program or a user can observe:

- bytes and schemas on disk, including bbolt, gob, JSON, localStorage, file
  names, modes, locking, migration, and recovery behavior;
- command names, arguments, flags, stdout, stderr, exit codes, ordering, and
  non-interactive guarantees;
- IPC method names, argument and result shapes, event names and payloads,
  frontend generation/revision fencing, and error strings where they cross
  the bridge;
- TUI and desktop navigation, keybindings, focus, rendering, terminal wire
  behavior, process lifecycle, accessibility, and recovery;
- authorization, redaction, bounds, checksums, signing, approval, audit, and
  other security properties; and
- package names and contents, platform handoff, installed identity, and
  supported release targets.

Internal Go APIs, package layout, goroutine structure, unobservable
algorithms, and source-code organization are out of scope. This document
does not prescribe the Rust crate graph, Tauri command implementation, a Go
sidecar, Ghostty, or the migration sequence in `docs/tauri-rust-recode.md`.
Features added after the release tag are also out of scope until this
matrix is deliberately revised.

The contract inventory was derived from the tagged source, Wails binding
surface, frontend call sites, tests, release workflow, and existing
acceptance/security documents. Surprising behavior is recorded as-is; this
is a compatibility baseline, not a design endorsement. The inventory was
initially cataloged in audit checkout `6e7f04c` and then reconciled against
the tag. Unqualified paths and line numbers locate semantically unchanged
code in that audit checkout. A `v0.21.0:` prefix locates tag-specific source
where later commits changed the behavior. Contract values always describe
the tag, regardless of reference form.

There are **762 contract rows**: 722 from the nine subsystem
inventories, 26 updater rows, and 14 packaging/release rows.
A row can overlap another subsystem's boundary; every row still requires its
own evidence because the producer and consumer may fail independently.

## Verification vocabulary

- **Existing automated test** names Go baseline coverage. The Rust rewrite
  needs an equivalent compatibility test or a cross-implementation fixture;
  the Go test alone does not prove Rust parity.
- **New automated test needed** means the behavior is visible in source but
  lacks a direct named baseline test. Add a Go golden/fixture where useful
  and a Rust parity test before closing the row.
- **Fixture/golden** requires durable Go freeze-snapshot input/output evidence, such
  as a database, gob value, JSON payload, command transcript, or rendered
  output, consumed or reproduced by the Rust implementation.
- **Manual acceptance** requires the repeatable native procedure identified
  by the row or the existing terminal/updater acceptance documents. Record
  only bounded, non-secret evidence.

### Manual procedure protocol

`MANUAL-<contract ID>` is the stable procedure identifier for every manual
row. On each applicable native target, build the exact candidate revision in
release mode; use a disposable project, home, repository, terminal, and
release fixture; exercise every success, refusal, timeout, recovery, and
lifecycle case stated in that row; and compare the observable result to the
row and its source. The row passes only when every stated value, byte shape,
ordering rule, bound, side effect, and security property matches. Store the
bounded result under the contract ID in the final parity report with the
candidate commit, OS/architecture, date, operator, and issue IDs. Never
record terminal content, credentials, tokens, authority-bearing URLs, or
private paths. Terminal rows additionally use `docs/terminal-acceptance.md`;
updater and release rows additionally use `docs/updater-acceptance.md`.

## CLI command surface

78 contracts. The table is both the compatibility specification and the parity acceptance matrix for this subsystem.

### Process-level conventions

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CLI-001` | CLI | Every subcommand is non-interactive (no prompts, no stdin reads except `capability mcp`); success exits 0, any error exits exactly 1 | New automated test needed | internal/cli/open.go:1-3 (package doc), main.go:44-47 |
| `CLI-002` | CLI | All errors are printed to **stderr only**, as the bare error message followed by `\n`, with no usage text and no `Error:` prefix (cobra `SilenceErrors`/`SilenceUsage` set recursively on the whole tree; main.go does `fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, err); os.Exit(1)`) | New automated test needed | main.go:44-47, internal/cli/root.go:40,67-79 |
| `CLI-003` | CLI | All normal output goes to **stdout** via `cmd.OutOrStdout()`; nothing writes to stderr except errors (and `commit show`'s passthrough of git's stderr, CLI-058) | Existing automated test — internal/cli/cli_test.go runCmd (captures out/err separately) | every command file (e.g. internal/cli/task.go:49), main.go:45 |
| `CLI-004` | CLI | Unknown command produces cobra's `unknown command "<x>" for "ptrack"` plus `Did you mean this?` suggestion block on stderr, exit 1 | New automated test needed | internal/cli/root.go:28-41 (no custom unknown-command handler; cobra default) |
| `CLI-005` | CLI | Argument-count violations produce cobra's standard messages (e.g. `accepts 1 arg(s), received 0`, `requires at least 1 arg(s), only received 0`, `unknown command ... for "ptrack"`) on stderr, exit 1, no usage text | New automated test needed | `Args:` validators throughout (e.g. internal/cli/task.go:24,115) + internal/cli/root.go:67-79 |
| `CLI-006` | CLI | `-h`/`--help` prints cobra-generated help to stdout, exit 0; root also gets `-v`/`--version` printing `ptrack version <v>` (cobra default from `root.Version`) | New automated test needed | internal/cli/root.go:29-41 |
| `CLI-007` | CLI | Cobra's implicit `completion` (bash/zsh/fish/powershell) and `help` commands exist and appear in root help output; they are NOT in the pinned subcommand list | New automated test needed | internal/cli/root.go:43-68 (no `CompletionOptions` disable) |
| `CLI-008` | CLI | Root command set is exactly these 21 named subcommands: init, goal, summary, milestone (alias ms), plan, task, issue, note, commit, hook, context, guide, next, search, board, gui, status, projects, backup, capability, version | Existing automated test (partial) — internal/cli/cli_test.go TestRootHasSubcommands (checks 16; exact set not asserted)<br>New automated test needed | internal/cli/root.go:43-65 |

## Storage and on-disk compatibility

50 contracts. The table is both the compatibility specification and the parity acceptance matrix for this subsystem.

### Database files & locations

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `STORE-001` | Storage | Project database lives at `<projectRoot>/.ptrack/ptrack.db` (dir `.ptrack`, file `ptrack.db`). | Fixture/golden — internal/store/discovery_test.go/TestInitAndFindSameDir | internal/store/discovery.go:13-17 |
| `STORE-002` | Storage | Global database lives at `$PTRACK_HOME/global.db` when `PTRACK_HOME` is set and non-empty, else `~/.ptrack/global.db`. The home dir is created with mode 0o755 on open. | Fixture/golden — internal/store/global_test.go/openGlobalTemp (uses PTRACK_HOME) | internal/store/global.go:26-48 |
| `STORE-003` | Storage | Both databases are bbolt (go.etcd.io/bbolt v1.5.0) single-file databases, opened with file mode 0o600. The Rust rewrite must either read/write the bbolt file format byte-compatibly or ship a one-way migration; coexistence with the Go binary requires the bbolt format. | Fixture/golden needed | internal/store/store.go:66, internal/store/global.go:48, go.mod:14 |
| `STORE-004` | Storage | Both DBs are opened with `bolt.Options{Timeout: time.Second}`: bbolt takes an exclusive `flock` on the file; a second process opening the same DB blocks up to 1s then fails. There is no inter-process shared access; concurrency within a process is bbolt's single-writer/multi-reader MVCC. | Manual acceptance `MANUAL-STORE-004` | internal/store/store.go:66, internal/store/global.go:48 |
| `STORE-005` | Storage | `FindProjectDB(start)` walks up from `start` (absolutized) returning the first existing `<dir>/.ptrack/ptrack.db` that is not a directory; it stops with `ErrNoProject` ("no ptrack project found (run 'ptrack init')") after inspecting a directory containing a `.git` marker (directory OR regular file — covers git worktrees) without a project DB, and at the filesystem root. | Existing automated test — TestFindFromNestedSubdir, TestFindNoProject, TestFindStopsAtGitBoundary, TestFindStopsAtGitWorktreeFileBoundary | internal/store/discovery.go:23-44,94-97 |
| `STORE-006` | Storage | `InitProject(dir)` errors with "ptrack project already exists at <path>" if `<dir>/.ptrack/ptrack.db` exists; otherwise creates `.ptrack` (0o755) and returns the DB path WITHOUT creating the file (file is created by the subsequent `Open`). Empty `dir` resolves to the enclosing git root if any, else the cwd. | Existing automated test — TestInitTwiceFails | internal/store/discovery.go:49-76 |
| `STORE-007` | Storage | `Store.ProjectRoot()` resolves the DB path through Abs+EvalSymlinks and requires the parent dir basename to be exactly `.ptrack`, else errors ("project database must be inside a .ptrack directory" / "project store is required" on nil). | New automated test needed | internal/store/store.go:84-101 |

The transaction is rolled back so the newer DB is left byte-untouched. | Existing automated test — version_test.go/TestRejectNewerFormat | internal/store/store.go:33-42,125-129 |
| `STORE-042` | Storage | Opening a DB with FormatVersion < CurrentFormat (including 0) silently migrates in place: bumps FormatVersion to CurrentFormat, sets LastWriteVersion and UpdatedAt; all data migration is shape-only (new buckets created by init; new gob fields decode as zero). No per-version transforms exist. | Existing automated test — version_test.go/TestAdoptPreVersioningDB, milestones_test.go/TestV1DBMigratesToV2, commits_test.go/TestV2DBMigratesToV3, version_test.go/TestMigrateV4AddsWritebackReceiptsAndPreservesLegacyNotes | internal/store/store.go:130-154 |

### Write semantics / observable mutation behavior

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `STORE-050` | Storage | Every mutation runs in a single bbolt read-write transaction; failure rolls back atomically (bbolt guarantee, relied on e.g. by WriteMemory target validation and ConvertTaskToPlan). There is no WAL/sidecar; crash recovery is bbolt's own meta-page double-buffering/freelist behavior. | Existing automated test — memory_writeback_test.go/TestWriteMemoryIdempotencyCollisionAndTargetValidationAreAtomic | internal/store/store.go:104,168,205 etc. |
| `STORE-051` | Storage | New plans/tasks/milestones get `Order = bucket.Stats().KeyN` at insert (key count BEFORE insert), Status active/todo/open respectively, CreatedAt=UpdatedAt=now (local time, `time.Now()` — not UTC except via WriteMemory which uses `.UTC()`). List functions sort by Order ascending with a stable sort, not by id. | Existing automated test — store_test.go/TestPlanCRUD, TestTaskCRUD | internal/store/store.go:203-240,281-302, internal/store/milestones.go:13-50, internal/store/helpers.go:40-43 |
| `STORE-052` | Storage | `AddTask`, `SetTaskPlan`, `SetActivePlan`, `SetPlanMilestone`, `AddIssue(taskID)` validate the referenced entity exists and return sentinel `ErrNotFound` ("not found") otherwise. GetX of a missing id also returns ErrNotFound. | Existing automated test — TestActivePlanRequiresExisting, TestSetTaskPlan, TestIssueCRUD | internal/store/store.go:16-17,193-198,284,398, internal/store/milestones.go:97-113,140 |
| `STORE-053` | Storage | All mutate paths bump `UpdatedAt = time.Now()` (local). `updateMeta` additionally stamps LastWriteVersion. | Existing automated test — TestMetaLifecycle | internal/store/store.go:167-179,273,527, internal/store/milestones.go:88,213 |
| `STORE-054` | Storage | `AddCommit` is idempotent by SHA: a linear scan over the commits bucket finds an existing record with equal SHA and returns it unchanged (no new id consumed). | Existing automated test — commits_test.go/TestCommitAddDedupAndLink | internal/store/commits.go:10-46 |
| `STORE-055` | Storage | `CompareAndSetTaskStatus` is a CAS fence: fails with error wrapping `ErrTaskStatusChanged` ("task status changed") and message `"task status changed: task #%d is plan #%d/%q at %s, expected plan #%d/%q at %s"` (times RFC3339Nano UTC) when plan id, status, or UpdatedAt (exact `time.Time.Equal`) differ; a no-op when current status already equals the target. | Existing automated test — store_test.go/TestCompareAndSetTaskStatusFencesPlanAndStatus | internal/store/store.go:19-21,353-387 |
| `STORE-056` | Storage | `ConvertTaskToPlan` in one transaction: new plan gets task's Title and CreatedAt, parent plan's MilestoneID, status done iff task was done else active; task's notes re-target to the plan; task's commits get TaskID=0/PlanID=newPlan; linked issues are unlinked (TaskID=0, UpdatedAt bumped); the task row is deleted. | Existing automated test — task_conversion_test.go/TestConvertTaskToPlanPreservesRelatedData, TestConvertDoneTaskCreatesDonePlan, TestConvertMissingTaskDoesNotCreatePlan | internal/store/store.go:412-517 |
| `STORE-057` | Storage | `AddIssue` defaults empty severity to "medium"; `AddCapability` defaults ModelVersion to 1 when 0 and forces Revision=1; `UpdateCapability` increments Revision, preserves ID/ModelVersion/CreatedAt, and any change to the security envelope (Kind, AgentProfile, ApprovalDurationSeconds, Limits, Audit policy, HTTP/Git/SSH scopes — compared via reflect.DeepEqual) force-disables the grant and zeroes ApprovedAt/ExpiresAt. | Existing automated test — TestIssueCRUD, capabilities_test.go/TestCapabilityMaterialEditRevokesApproval, TestCapabilityAuditPolicyEditRevokesApproval | internal/store/milestones.go:132-158, internal/store/capabilities.go:11-76,90-112 |
| `STORE-058` | Storage | `DeleteCapability` removes the grant but deliberately retains its audit records (tombstone history); audits remain listable by capability id. | Existing automated test — capabilities_test.go/TestCapabilityLifecycleAndAuditRetention | internal/store/capabilities.go:78-88 |
| `STORE-059` | Storage | Capability audit pruning: `AddCapabilityAuditBounded` prunes in the same transaction — newest-first scan, drop when total>totalKeep (>0) or per-capability matches>perCapabilityKeep (>0); non-positive ceilings = unlimited. `PruneCapabilityAudits(id, keep)` clamps negative keep to 0. | Existing automated test — capabilities_test.go/TestCapabilityLifecycleAndAuditRetention | internal/store/capabilities.go:114-196 |

### Memory write-back (v5)

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `STORE-060` | Storage | `WriteMemory` validates before touching the DB: RequestID 1–128 chars, charset `[A-Za-z0-9._:-]`; Body non-empty valid UTF-8; WorkspaceGeneration≠0, SessionID 1–128 chars, AssociationRevision≠0; Kind ∈ {summary, decision, blocker, handoff}. Violations return errors wrapping `ErrInvalidMemoryWriteback` ("invalid memory write-back") with suffixes ": invalid request ID" / ": content is required" / ": source association is required" / `: unsupported kind %q`. | Existing automated test (partial) — memory_writeback_test.go (indirect)<br>New automated test needed | internal/store/memory_writeback.go:22-28,142-168 |
| `STORE-061` | Storage | Idempotency: replay record key = RequestID; digest = SHA-256 of the JSON object `{"kind","body","target","target_id","plan_id","generation","session_id","revision"}` (Go encoding/json field order as declared). Same RequestID + same digest → replayed result (no mutation; returns stored note or the request body for summaries). Same RequestID + different digest → `ErrMemoryWritebackReplay` ("memory write-back request ID was already used"). | Existing automated test — memory_writeback_test.go/TestWriteMemoryIdempotencyCollisionAndTargetValidationAreAtomic | internal/store/memory_writeback.go:64-94,199-218 |
| `STORE-062` | Storage | Target revalidation inside the write tx: project target requires TargetID=PlanID=0; plan target requires PlanID==TargetID and the plan exists; task target requires task exists, task.PlanID==PlanID, and plan exists — else ErrInvalidMemoryWriteback with ": invalid project target" / ": plan target no longer exists" / ": invalid task target" / ": task target no longer exists" / ": task target changed plans" / `: unsupported target %q`. A failed validation writes nothing. | Existing automated test — TestWriteMemoryIdempotencyCollisionAndTargetValidationAreAtomic, TestWriteMemorySerializesWithConcurrentTaskConversion | internal/store/memory_writeback.go:170-197 |
| `STORE-063` | Storage | Summary writes replace Meta.Summary (meta.UpdatedAt/LastWriteVersion stamped, times in UTC here unlike other paths); decision/blocker/handoff writes create a Note with Kind set and CreatedAt UTC. Replay receipts are pruned to at most 256 records (MemoryWritebackReplayLimit), deleting the lowest Sequence, after each write. | Existing automated test — TestWriteMemoryKindsScopesAndSummaryReplacement, TestWriteMemoryReplayReceiptsAreBounded | internal/store/memory_writeback.go:16-20,99-140,220-245 |

## Domain model invariants

40 contracts. The table is both the compatibility specification and the parity acceptance matrix for this subsystem.

### Serialization & storage representation

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MODEL-001` | Domain model | All persistent entities are serialized with Go `encoding/gob` into bbolt buckets; the structs carry no behavior beyond their fields. ## Terminal user interface

58 contracts. The table is both the compatibility specification and the parity acceptance matrix for this subsystem.

### Entry / exit / process behavior

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TUI-001` | TUI | Running `ptrack` with no subcommand launches the TUI (desktop build: `main.go:36-43` overrides `cli.RunNoArgs` to call `tui.Run()`). In builds without the desktop tag, the same invocation instead prints `ptrack: nothing to do. Run 'ptrack --help' for commands or 'ptrack status' for an overview.` to stdout and exits 0 (`internal/cli/root.go:12,21-25`). | Manual acceptance `MANUAL-TUI-001` | main.go:36-43, internal/cli/root.go:12,21-25 |
| `TUI-002` | TUI | Project discovery before launch: walk up from the process CWD looking for `<dir>/.ptrack/ptrack.db`; stop (with `ErrNoProject`) after a directory containing `.git` or at filesystem root (`internal/store/discovery.go:23-44`, called from `internal/tui/tui.go:27-34`). | New automated test needed | internal/store/discovery.go:23-44, internal/tui/tui.go:27-34 |
| `TUI-003` | TUI | Outside any project, `ptrack` (no args) prints the exact getting-started hint to **stdout** and exits **0** — no error, no stderr (`main.go:38-41`; hint text verbatim in `internal/cli/hint.go:5-14`: `p-track  ·  persistent project memory`, rule line, `No p-track project here yet.`, `GET STARTED` block listing `ptrack init`, `ptrack init --goal "..."`, `ptrack --help`, and closing `Once a project exists, run \`ptrack\` to open the dashboard.`). | Fixture/golden needed | main.go:38-41, internal/cli/hint.go:5-14 |
| `TUI-004` | TUI | Any other `tui.Run()` error propagates to `main`, is printed to stderr, and the process exits 1 (`main.go:44-47`). Quitting the TUI normally (`q`/`ctrl+c`) exits 0 with no output. | Manual acceptance `MANUAL-TUI-004` | main.go:44-47 |
| `TUI-005` | TUI | Before launching, `store.WriterVersion` is set to the CLI version string so the DB records the last writer (`main.go:24`). | New automated test needed | main.go:24 |
| `TUI-006` | TUI | The DB is opened once at startup to validate/migrate, then closed immediately (`internal/tui/tui.go:36-40`); the running TUI never holds the bbolt file lock while idle — every store operation opens and closes the DB (`internal/tui/conn.go:14-21`). A concurrent agent/CLI can open and write the same DB while the TUI is alive. | Existing automated test — `tui_test.go:TestIdleModelHoldsNoLock` | internal/tui/tui.go:36-40, internal/tui/conn.go:14-21 |
| `TUI-007` | TUI | The TUI runs in the terminal alternate screen (full-screen app, `tea.WithAltScreen()` at `internal/tui/tui.go:46`); on exit the previous terminal content is restored. | Manual acceptance `MANUAL-TUI-007` | internal/tui/tui.go:46 |
| `TUI-008` | TUI | On launch the model loads a full snapshot: meta, milestones, plans, tasks grouped by plan, issues, counts; the plan cursor is initialized to the active plan's position in the plan list (`internal/tui/tui.go:131-143,145-172`). | Existing automated test (partial) — `tui_test.go:TestIdleModelHoldsNoLock` (partial)<br>New automated test needed | internal/tui/tui.go:131-143,145-172 |

## GUI bridge and desktop lifecycle

136 contracts. The table is both the compatibility specification and the parity acceptance matrix for this subsystem.

### A. Bound methods — transport & shared fencing

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GUI-001` | GUI bridge | `runWails` binds one `*gui.App`; Wails v2.13.0 exposes its 63 exported methods as Promise-returning `window.go.gui.App.<Method>` calls. The frontend `api()` rejects locally when the backend object is absent. A Go error rejects with an `Error` whose `.message` contains the backend error text; successful structs use Go JSON field tags. `GetUpdateState`, `GetWorkspaceState`, `CancelUpdateOperation`, and `InstallShellCommand` have no application-level error return, although transport failure remains possible. Generated wrappers are build products and are absent from the tag tree. | Existing automated test (partial) — frontend/src/build.test.js:44-49 (five updater names only; exact export set and transport semantics unpinned)<br>New automated test needed | v0.21.0:internal/gui/run.go:55-78, v0.21.0:frontend/src/app.js:494-498, v0.21.0:go.mod:13, v0.21.0:internal/gui (63 exported `*App` methods) |
| `GUI-002` | GUI bridge | Shared fencing vocabulary for every generation-scoped method: no open workspace → `no project workspace is open`; non-zero generation mismatch → `stale workspace generation: expected <exp>, active <act>` (decimal, space-separated); closing workspace → `workspace is closing`; resource-admission fence (only reachable from `CreateTerminalV2` and `LaunchLinkedAgentV2`) → `workspace resource admission is fenced`. Generation `0` disables the equality check in both `currentWorkspace` and `beginOperation`. | Existing automated test — internal/gui/workspace_lifecycle_test.go/TestWorkspaceCoordinatorRejectsStaleGeneration, internal/gui/workspace_context_test.go/TestWorkspaceContextGenerationAndResourceFence | internal/gui/workspace_lifecycle.go:26,122-138, internal/gui/workspace_context.go:14-16,136-161 |
| `GUI-003` | GUI bridge | Terminal-family methods additionally gate on app lifecycle: before `onStartup` completes or after shutdown begins they fail with `terminal lifecycle is shutting down`; a nil terminal manager yields `terminal manager is unavailable`. | Existing automated test — internal/gui/terminal_test.go/TestTerminalShutdownIsIdempotent, TestCreateTerminalWaitsForStartupBeforeCreatingAndEmitting | internal/gui/terminal.go:931-966 |
| `GUI-004` | GUI bridge | V1 methods are thin delegates to their V2/V3 counterparts with generation `0` (no generation check, otherwise identical semantics): `GetBoard`→`getBoard(0,…)`, `AddTask`→`addTask(0,…)`, `RenameTask`→`renameTask(0,…)`, `MoveTask`→`moveTask(0,…)`, `AddTaskNote`→`addTaskNote(0,…)` (app.go); `GetTerminalProfiles`→`GetTerminalProfilesV2(0)`, `CreateTerminal`→`CreateTerminalV2(0,…)` (still resource-admission fenced), `ResizeTerminal`→`ResizeTerminalV2(0,…)`, `CloseTerminal`→`CloseTerminalV2(0,…)` (terminal.go:310,355,633,654). | Existing automated test — internal/gui/terminal_test.go/TestResizeAndCloseTerminalDelegateOrderingForceAndErrors, internal/gui/app_test.go/TestGetBoardV2ResolvesCurrentGenerationForZero | internal/gui/app.go:199-201,463-466,502-505,539-542,566-569, internal/gui/terminal.go:310-313,355-372,633-635,654-656 |

| `GUI-054` | GUI bridge | `PreviewTerminalWritebackV2(generation uint64, sessionID string, expectedRevision uint64, kind string, content string) (TerminalWritebackPreviewV2, error)` — validation errors (all exact): unsupported kind → `write-back content is invalid: unsupported type` (valid kinds: summary/decision/blocker/handoff); invalid UTF-8 → `write-back content is invalid: content must be valid UTF-8`; empty after CRLF→LF normalization + trim → `write-back content is invalid: content is required`; over limits (8 KiB bytes / 4000 runes / 128 lines) → `write-back content is invalid: content exceeds the hard limit`; control chars other than \n/\t → `write-back content is invalid: content contains unsupported characters`; credential-looking content → `write-back content may contain a credential` (content never echoed in errors). Echoes the **normalized** content back (`content`, `contentBytes`); `destination` is `Plan #<id>` / `Task #<id>` / `Project`, overridden to `Project rolling summary` for kind summary; `replacesSummary` = kind==summary. | Existing automated test — internal/gui/terminal_writeback_test.go/TestTerminalWritebackDerivesTaskTargetAndReplaysWithoutDuplicate, TestTerminalWritebackSupportsPlanProjectAndExplicitSummary, TestTerminalWritebackRejectsStaleDetachedClosedAndMovedTargets, TestTerminalWritebackRejectsCredentialsAndHardBoundsWithoutEcho | internal/gui/terminal_writeback.go:18-26,67-112,195-294 |

| `GUI-070` | GUI bridge | DTO mechanics: every wire type is a generated TS class with `static createFrom(source)` and a constructor assigning `this.x = source["x"]` (nested structs via `convertValues`, which returns falsy input unchanged — missing nested objects stay null/undefined). `?` in TS = Go pointer/`omitempty` (key may be absent); `uint64`/`int` → `number`; `time.Time` → RFC3339/RFC3339Nano string (except `model.Capability`/`CapabilityAudit` time fields typed `any` with passthrough). Casing is per-field and mixed: `model.*`, `capability.ConnectionDiagnostic`, and `gui.CapabilityView.effective_scope` use snake_case; everything else camelCase. 103 classes across 7 namespaces (agentrun 1, association 3, capability 1, gitinfo 10, gui 79, model 7, terminal 2); `gui.AgentWorkflowKind` is referenced by App.d.ts:98 but **not defined** in models.ts (plain string on the wire). | Fixture/golden — generated code + frontend/src/build.test.js | frontend/wailsjs/go/models.ts:1-3206 |
| `GUI-071` | GUI bridge | `association.PointerV1` {version:number; planId?:number; taskId?:number} — detach/project pointer is exactly `{version:1}`; zero plan+task = project context. `association.TargetV1` {planId?:number; taskId?:number}. `association.AssociationV1` {version:number; projectRoot:string; generation:number; liveId:string; target:TargetV1; revision:number}. `VersionV1 = 1`. ### C. Events (Go → frontend)

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GUI-096` | GUI bridge | Event `terminal:status` — payload `TerminalStatus` {generation:number; sessionId:string; state:string}. Emitted on CreateTerminalV2/LaunchLinkedAgentV2 success, CloseTerminalV2/RollbackLinkedAgentLaunchV2 success (state `closed`), and attachment-lease expiry (state `closed`). Gated by `workspaceIsPublished` (same workspace pointer + unchanged generation). **No frontend subscriber exists** (only tests pin it). | Existing automated test — internal/gui/terminal_test.go/TestTerminalLifecycleRetainsStartupContextAndEmitsOnlyTypedStatusAndExit | internal/gui/terminal.go:805-807,568-572,625-629,686-691, internal/gui/linked_launch.go:226-230,281-285 |
| `GUI-097` | GUI bridge | Event `terminal:exit` — payload `TerminalExit` {generation:number; sessionId:string; exitCode:number; state:string; error?:string} with `error` = the process error's `Error()` text verbatim. Emitted by the exit monitor **before** a trailing `workspace:runtime-changed`; the monitor also revokes the capability session and records the agent-run result text `exited`/`failed`. Frontend consumption: `frontend/src/terminal/pane.ts:601-603` — drops payloads without sessionId, drops when `generation ≠ 0 && generation ≠ workspaceGeneration`, caches early exits (exit-before-stream-connect). | Existing automated test — internal/gui/terminal_test.go/TestTerminalLifecycleRetainsStartupContextAndEmitsOnlyTypedStatusAndExit, frontend/src/terminal/pane.test.ts | internal/gui/terminal.go:809-811,872-916 |
| `GUI-098` | GUI bridge | Event `workspace:runtime-changed` — constant defined at internal/gui/watch.go:15; payload is a **bare uint64 generation** (no envelope). Emitted once per successful runtime mutation (associations, ownership, worktrees, handoffs, workflows, linked launch/rollback, terminal exit) and by the agent registry's runtime-changed callback; gated by `workspaceIsPublished`. Frontend: `frontend/src/app.js:4389` — accepted only when payload > 0 and == current generation and status `open`, then coalesced at 150 ms. | Existing automated test — internal/gui/association_mutation_test.go/TestMutateTerminalAssociationPublishesOneGenerationScopedRuntimeEvent, agent_coordination_acceptance_test.go/TestAgentCoordinationRuntimeNotificationIsPublishedGenerationScoped, frontend/src/workspace/presentation.test.ts:409 | internal/gui/terminal.go:831-835, internal/gui/agent_runs.go:104-115, internal/gui/watch.go:15 |
| `GUI-099` | GUI bridge | Event `workspace:data-changed` — payload: bare uint64 generation captured when the watcher (re)started (stale-tolerant: not re-read at emit time). Filesystem watcher polls the project DB file every 2 s (fingerprint {exists, size, modTime}; stat error counts as a change, so deletion is detected) and emits once per burst, 500 ms after the last observed change. Not started before `onStartup`; cancelled on workspace switch/close/shutdown. Frontend: `frontend/src/app.js:4386-4388` — payload ignored, triggers a quiet snapshot reload. | Existing automated test — internal/gui/watch_test.go/TestWatchWorkspaceDataDebouncesChangeBursts, TestWatchWorkspaceDataDetectsRemoval | internal/gui/watch.go:9-19,22-127 |
| `GUI-100` | GUI bridge | Event `update:state-changed` — payload: full `UpdateState` struct (GUI-094). Emitted at update.go:151 (preference set), :271 (download ready), :330 (apply result), :346 (cancel — always, even no-op), :393 (startup recovery), :469 (operation entry), :511 (check outcome), :541/:559 (progress/completion ticks). Frontend: `frontend/src/app.js:4385` + `updates/presentation.js:9-11` — events with `revision` lower than the current one are **silently dropped**; unknown phase renders fail-closed. | Existing automated test — internal/gui/update_test.go (multiple), frontend/src/updates/presentation.test.js:52-112, frontend/src/build.test.js:56-61 | internal/gui/update.go:18 + emission sites |
| `GUI-101` | GUI bridge | Native-menu events are exactly five names, all emitted with nil payload: `workspace:open-requested`, `workspace:switch-requested`, `workspace:close-requested`, `workspace:capabilities-requested`, and `update:open-requested`. The tag frontend subscribes to those names directly in `app.js`; there is no native-menu adapter. | Existing automated test (partial) — v0.21.0:internal/gui/project_menu_test.go/TestProjectWorkspaceMenuExposesLifecycleActions (all five names; nil payload unpinned)<br>New automated test needed | v0.21.0:internal/gui/project_menu.go:10-50, v0.21.0:frontend/src/app.js:4121-4134 |
| `GUI-102` | GUI bridge | Event emission is best-effort: `emitTerminalEvent` snapshots the Wails context and emitter under the lifecycle mutex, then silently drops the event when either is absent; it does not separately fence shutdown, frontend close, or in-flight runtime calls. Production transport is `wailsruntime.EventsEmit(ctx, name, payload)`. The frontend subscribes via `window.runtime.EventsOnMultiple(name, cb, -1)` and never emits Wails events (Go→JS only). | New automated test needed | v0.21.0:internal/gui/terminal.go:803-812, v0.21.0:internal/gui/run.go:18-23, v0.21.0:frontend/src/app.js:4121-4125 |

| `GUI-108` | GUI bridge | `onShutdown` is idempotent. It marks shutdown started, clears any pending workspace confirmation, detaches the workspace, cancels monitoring, closes the workspace, stops its watcher, and waits up to 3 seconds each for terminal operations, update operations, and monitor goroutines. It does not clear/fence Wails runtime calls or wait for an in-flight runtime-call group. More than 64 blocks with `Too many saved updates require manual cleanup.`; unreadable/invalid and wrong-host stages are skipped. An unresolved pending-apply recovery error or any remaining Linux `.pending-apply-*.json` blocks with `A previous update requires manual recovery.` The newest valid same-host stage newer than current becomes `ready` (ties keep the first name-sorted), with release only `{version,sizeBytes}` and checksumVerified true; other valid stages are discarded. No candidate settles to `idle`. ## Embedded terminal

108 contracts. The table is both the compatibility specification and the parity acceptance matrix for this subsystem.

### Stream server & wire protocol (WebSocket)

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TERM-001` | Terminal | Each terminal Manager owns exactly one TCP4 loopback listener bound to `127.0.0.1:0` (OS-assigned port, IPv4 only); all sessions of that manager share it. | Existing automated test — TestStreamServerUsesOneOSAssignedLoopbackListenerPerManager | internal/terminal/server.go:48 |
| `TERM-002` | Terminal | Stream URLs are `ws://127.0.0.1:<port>/terminal/<sessionID>?token=<streamToken>`; path prefix constant `/terminal/`. | Existing automated test — TestStreamServerUsesOneOSAssignedLoopbackListenerPerManager | internal/terminal/server.go:19,84-91 |
| `TERM-003` | Terminal | Session IDs and stream tokens are independent 32-byte crypto-random values, base64url-encoded without padding (`base64.RawURLEncoding`, 43 chars); the shell-integration nonce is a third independent value. | New automated test needed | internal/terminal/manager.go:148-162,436-442 |
| `TERM-004` | Terminal | HTTP gate before upgrade: non-GET or disallowed Origin → `403 "terminal stream rejected"`; empty session ID or ID containing `/` → 404; unknown session → 404; missing/wrong token → `401 "terminal stream rejected"` (constant-time compare via `subtle.ConstantTimeCompare`); second concurrent attach → `409 "terminal stream unavailable"`; server stopping → `503 "terminal stream server is stopping"`. | Existing automated test — TestStreamServerRejectsMissingWrongOrUnknownAuthentication, TestStreamServerRejectsSecondAttachment, TestStreamServerRejectsClosedSessionAndItsExpiredURL | internal/terminal/server.go:93-146, session.go:348-365 |
| `TERM-005` | Terminal | Origin allowlist: reject empty/`"null"` origins, origins with userinfo, and non-http(s) schemes; accept `wails://wails`; accept `http(s)://wails.localhost`; accept http(s) origins whose hostname is `localhost` (case-insensitive) or a loopback IP literal (any port). Lookalike hosts (`wails.localhost.evil.example`) and `file://` are rejected. | Existing automated test — TestStreamServerOriginPolicy | internal/terminal/server.go:304-327 |
| `TERM-006` | Terminal | HTTP/WebSocket timeouts: `ReadHeaderTimeout` 5s, handshake timeout 10s, per-write deadline 10s, server ping every 25s, read deadline (pong wait) 60s refreshed by pongs. | Manual acceptance `MANUAL-TERM-006` | internal/terminal/server.go:20-23,62-71,190-191,262-268,297-302 |
| `TERM-007` | Terminal | Server→client terminal output is binary WebSocket frames of at most 64 KiB (`outputChunkBytes = 64*1024`); startup replay and live output are split into ≤64 KiB chunks; empty chunks are never sent. | Existing automated test — TestSplitOutputCapsChunksAt64KiB, TestStreamServerCarriesOnlyBinaryPTYInputAndOutput | internal/terminal/protocol.go:15,196-207, session.go:518 |
| `TERM-008` | Terminal | Flow control: server keeps at most 512 KiB (`outputWindowBytes`) outstanding; before each PTY read it reserves one 64 KiB slot, and reservations may fill at most window−chunk (448 KiB) so one max chunk is always in flight; a client ACK releases capacity exactly. | Existing automated test — TestStreamServerCapsUnacknowledgedOutputAndResumesAfterACK, TestFlowLedgerLeavesRoomForOneInflightMaximumChunkAndResumesAfterACK, TestFlowLedgerConcurrentReservationsRemainWithinBound | internal/terminal/protocol.go:16,108-128, server.go:157,196-216,232-255 |
| `TERM-009` | Terminal | Client→server ACK is a text frame with strict JSON `{"type":"ack","bytes":N}`: object only, both fields required, `type` must be `"ack"`, `bytes` a positive base-10 integer with no leading zero/sign/fraction/exponent; unknown or duplicate fields, empty payload, payloads >1024 bytes (`maxControlFrameBytes`), and trailing data are all rejected and terminate the connection. | Existing automated test — TestParseACKControlAcceptsOnlyStrictPositiveIntegerACK, TestParseACKControlRejectsMalformedOrNonStrictFrames, TestParseACKControlRejectsOversizedFrame | internal/terminal/protocol.go:17,21-91 |
| `TERM-010` | Terminal | ACK of zero/negative bytes or more bytes than the server has sent is an error and closes the stream. | Existing automated test — TestFlowLedgerRejectsACKBeyondBytesActuallySent | internal/terminal/protocol.go:146-162 |
| `TERM-011` | Terminal | Client→server terminal input is raw binary frames (no envelope); each must be non-empty and ≤64 KiB (`maxInputFrameBytes`, also enforced as the WS read limit); any other frame type (ping/pong handled by library, close, continuation) or an invalid text frame closes the connection. | Existing automated test — TestStreamServerRejectsMalformedControlAndOversizedFrames, TestStreamServerCarriesOnlyBinaryPTYInputAndOutput | internal/terminal/server.go:18,257-295 |
| `TERM-012` | Terminal | When the session's live output ends (process exited and drained), the server sends a WebSocket close frame with code 1000 (normal closure) and empty reason. | New automated test needed | internal/terminal/server.go:197-206 |
| `TERM-013` | Terminal | Any stream connection termination — clean close, abrupt disconnect, protocol violation — causes the server to gracefully close the owning session (`CloseSession(id, false)`): the shell process is terminated, and the session ID/URL become permanently unusable (no reconnect, no re-attach). | Existing automated test — TestStreamDisconnectClosesSessionAndPreventsReconnect | internal/terminal/server.go:103-112,148-170 |
| `TERM-014` | Terminal | Sustained throughput is lossless under the ACK regime: verified end-to-end with 100 MiB in deterministic ≤64 KiB binary frames. | Existing automated test — TestStreamServerSustains100MiBWithAcknowledgements | internal/terminal/server.go (whole), protocol.go |

### PTY session lifecycle

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TERM-015` | Terminal | Session state machine strings: `"starting"`, `"running"`, `"exited"`, `"closing"`, `"closed"`, `"failed"` (type `SessionState`). | Existing automated test — TestSessionStateValues | internal/terminal/session.go:14-23 |
| `TERM-016` | Terminal | Normal lifecycle: starting → running → exited → closed; start failure → failed; read/stream failure → failed (plus process kill). Exit results carry `{ExitCode, State, Err}` on a buffered(1) channel that is closed after exactly one delivery. | Existing automated test — TestSessionTransitionsStartingRunningExitedClosed, TestSessionStartFailureTransitionsToFailed | internal/terminal/session.go:129-172,474-514,581-593 |
| `TERM-017` | Terminal | Exit code semantics: the child's real exit code is reported; a nonzero exit status is an ordinary `exited` result with no error; only a wait failure that produced no process state yields exit code −1 with an error (→ `failed`). | Existing automated test — TestNativePTYInteractiveUnicodeResizeAndExit (code 7), TestSessionTransitionsStartingRunningExitedClosed (code 23) | internal/terminal/gopty.go:91-104, session.go:488-513 |
| `TERM-018` | Terminal | Terminal dimensions are clamped to 1..1000 rows and 1..1000 columns both at session creation (before PTY start) and on every resize; identical-dimension resizes are no-ops that never reach the PTY; resizing a non-running session errors `resize terminal session in state %q`. | Existing automated test — TestSessionClampsInitialDimensionsBeforePTYStart, TestSessionResizeClampsDimensionsAndSkipsIdenticalRequests | internal/terminal/session.go:29-31,111,367-384,629-633 |
| `TERM-019` | Terminal | Input writes: full-buffer write loop to the PTY (short writes retried, zero-progress → `io.ErrShortWrite`); writing a non-running session errors `write terminal input in state %q`; each write bumps `lastActivityAt`. | New automated test needed | internal/terminal/session.go:386-407 |
| `TERM-020` | Terminal | Startup buffer: before the first stream attach, up to 64 KiB (`defaultStartupBufferBytes`) of output is retained; once full, PTY reads stall (applying backpressure to the child) until attach or close; on attach the buffered prefix is delivered first, then live output streams. | Existing automated test — TestSessionRetainsBoundedStartupOutputAndForwardsLiveOutputAfterAttach | internal/terminal/session.go:26,534-553,562-579 |
| `TERM-021` | Terminal | Attachment lease: a session supports exactly one attach; `ExpireUnattached` atomically competes with attach (exactly one winner). The GUI host enforces a 30 s lease: an unclaimed session is force-closed, its capability/event tokens revoked first, and a `terminal:status` `closed` event is published. | Existing automated test — TestSessionAttachmentLeaseAttachAndExpiryHaveOneWinner, gui:TestTerminalAttachmentLeaseExpiresUnclaimedSessionAfterRevocation, gui:TestTerminalAttachmentCancelsLeaseAndWorkspaceShutdownOwnsUnclaimedCleanup | internal/terminal/session.go:329-365, internal/gui/terminal.go:580-631, internal/gui/workspace_lifecycle.go:87 |
| `TERM-022` | Terminal | Natural exit drain: after the child exits, the reader has up to 250 ms (`defaultOutputDrainTimeout`) to deliver remaining PTY output before the PTY is closed; all final bytes must reach the attached consumer before the exit result is emitted. | Existing automated test — TestSessionWaitsForFinalOutputBeforeClosingPTY, TestSessionBoundsNaturalExitDrainBeforeClosingPTY, TestSessionNaturalExitDrainsPTYOutput | internal/terminal/session.go:28,474-514 |
| `TERM-023` | Terminal | Graceful close (`force=false`): Terminate → wait up to 750 ms (`defaultGracefulTimeout`) for exit → Kill if still alive → close PTY → wait for workers → state `closed`. Force close (`force=true`): Kill immediately, no graceful window. `Close` is idempotent (single `closeOnce`). | Existing automated test — TestSessionGracefulCloseTerminatesWithoutKillWhenProcessExits, TestSessionGracefulCloseTransitionsClosingThenKillsAfterInjectedTimeout, TestSessionForceCloseKillsImmediatelyWithoutGracefulTimeout | internal/terminal/session.go:27,409-472 |
| `TERM-024` | Terminal | Unix Terminate/Kill target the child's whole process group (`kill(-pgid, SIGTERM/SIGKILL)`, ESRCH tolerated); closing the PTY joins master+slave closes and also SIGKILLs the group, so descendants are reaped on force close, natural exit, and read failure. PTY read `EIO` is normalized to `io.EOF`. | Existing automated test — TestNativePTYForceCloseKillsDescendantProcessGroup, TestNativePTYNaturalExitKillsRemainingDescendantProcessGroup, TestNormalizePTYReadErrorTreatsEIOAsEOF | internal/terminal/gopty_unix.go:28-42,44-89,91-124 |
| `TERM-025` | Terminal | Windows: ConPTY process is created `CREATE_SUSPENDED`, assigned to a Job Object with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, then resumed; Terminate closes the pseudoconsole (sends CTRL_CLOSE_EVENT); Kill uses `TerminateJobObject(job, 1)` (falling back to `process.Kill`); closing the job handle kills the tree. | Existing automated test — TestNativePTYForceCloseKillsDescendantJob, TestNativePTYNaturalExitKillsRemainingDescendantJob, TestNativePTYInteractiveUnicodeResizeAndExit | internal/terminal/gopty_windows.go:26-171 |
| `TERM-026` | Terminal | Failed PTY start cleanup: kill + close + wait, bounded by a 5 s timeout; the failed session is never registered in the manager. | Existing automated test — TestManagerCreateFailureIsCleanedUpAndNotRetained | internal/terminal/gopty.go:54,128-146, session.go:137-147, manager.go:190-207 |
| `TERM-027` | Terminal | PTY start passes executable/args/env/CWD/rows/columns verbatim from the validated launch request (defensive copies; caller mutation after Create cannot leak in). | Existing automated test — TestSessionPassesRequestedStartDataToPTY, TestManagerOwnsSessionsAndStartsWithCopiedProfileParameters | internal/terminal/pty.go:7-34, session.go:95-127, manager_test.go |

## Capabilities

93 contracts. The table is both the compatibility specification and the parity acceptance matrix for this subsystem.

### HTTP scope normalization

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CAP-008` | Capabilities | Base URL must be an absolute hierarchical `http`/`https` URL (`url.ParseRequestURI`, non-opaque), no userinfo, no query or fragment, no control characters; scheme lowercased; default ports (:80/:443) stripped. Errors: `URL must be an absolute hierarchical URL`, `URL scheme must be http or https`, `embedded URL credentials are not allowed`, `HTTP base URL cannot contain a query or fragment`. **Reviewed Rust security compatibility waiver:** affected targets are Rust HTTP normalization on macOS, Linux, and Windows. Observed difference: browser-style IPv4 shorthand/numeric forms and mixed ASCII/non-ASCII IDN labels are rejected rather than coerced or accepted. User impact: drafts or existing grants using those ambiguous host spellings fail preview/authorization and must be rewritten with an explicit canonical IP or unambiguous DNS label. Security/data-compatibility risk: fail-closed denial may make such grants temporarily unusable, while accepting alternate host interpretations risks origin confusion and approval bypass; stored records remain readable but unusable until edited and explicitly re-approved. Rationale: preserve the deny-by-default origin boundary across parsers. Approved by the Plan #8 owner's end-to-end and deny-by-default directive; tracking: Plan #8 task #69; expiry/removal condition: remove only after the Go contract adopts the same rejection or a reviewed replacement parser proves one canonical cross-language authority representation and updates the Go/Rust fixtures. | Existing automated test — TestNormalizeRejectsCredentialsAndAmbiguousHTTPPaths (normalize_test.go:53) | internal/capability/normalize.go:167-177,208-245,247-263 |
| `CAP-009` | Capabilities | URL paths are decoded and re-canonicalized; encoded `%2f`, `%5c`, `%2e` (any case) are rejected outright, and decoded paths containing `\`, `%`, or control chars are rejected (`encoded slash, backslash, or dot path segments are not allowed`, `URL path is ambiguous`); final path is `path.Clean`-ed with `RawPath` cleared. **Reviewed Rust security compatibility waiver:** affected targets are Rust HTTP scope normalization on macOS, Linux, and Windows. Observed difference: decoded control characters are also rejected in approved path prefixes. User impact: a previously stored prefix containing a percent-encoded control is invalid and requires removal plus explicit re-approval. Security/data-compatibility risk: fail-closed denial can interrupt that grant; accepting invisible delimiters creates audit/display ambiguity and path-policy disagreement. Rationale: the persisted approval text and runtime path must have one printable interpretation. Approved by the Plan #8 owner's end-to-end and deny-by-default directive; tracking: Plan #8 task #69; expiry/removal condition: remove only if Go rejects the same inputs or a reviewed encoding contract safely represents controls identically in normalization, display, audit, and authorization fixtures. | Existing automated test — TestThreatHTTPEncodedPathEscapesAreDenied (threat_test.go:11), TestNormalizeRejectsCredentialsAndAmbiguousHTTPPaths | internal/capability/normalize.go:235-244,280-286 |
| `CAP-010` | Capabilities | Methods are uppercased and must be in {GET, HEAD, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE, OPTIONS}; at least one is required (`HTTP method %q is not supported`, `HTTP scope must approve at least one method`). | Existing automated test — TestHTTPWriteMethodAndPathRequireExactApproval (policy_test.go:138) | internal/capability/normalize.go:163-165,178-189 |
| `CAP-011` | Capabilities | Path prefixes must be absolute, query/fragment-free, unambiguous (same encoding rules as CAP-009), and each must lie within the base URL path on a **segment boundary** (`pathWithin`: equal, or parent `/`, or child has prefix `parent+"/"`); an empty prefix list defaults to `[base.Path]`. | Existing automated test — TestNormalizeHTTPPathUsesSegmentBoundaries (normalize_test.go:69) | internal/capability/normalize.go:190-205,265-278,288-292 |
| `CAP-012` | Capabilities | HTTP effective-scope string format: `"<METHODS> <baseURL> paths=<p1,p2>"` (comma-joined, sorted). | Existing automated test — TestNormalizeHTTPDisplaysCanonicalEffectiveScope | internal/capability/normalize.go:205 |

### Git scope normalization

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CAP-013` | Capabilities | Git remote URL accepts `https://`, `ssh://`, or SCP-like `[user@]host:path` syntax only; scheme lowercased; embedded credentials rejected (`embedded Git credentials are not allowed`) except an `ssh://` username without password; query/fragment rejected; default ports stripped (:22 for ssh); SCP form re-emitted as `user@host:path` with leading `/` trimmed. **Reviewed Rust security compatibility waiver:** affected targets are Rust Git remote normalization on macOS, Linux, and Windows. Observed difference: browser-style IPv4 shorthand/numeric forms, mixed ASCII/non-ASCII IDN labels, and scheme-looking values missing `://` are rejected rather than reinterpreted as another authority or SCP syntax. User impact: ambiguous existing remotes fail authorization and must be rewritten canonically and explicitly re-approved. Security/data-compatibility risk: fail-closed denial may interrupt fetch/push, while accepting parser-dependent authority or syntax interpretation could send Git traffic outside the approved remote. Rationale: the digest, fresh remote verification, and executor must share an exact authority string. Approved by the Plan #8 owner's end-to-end and deny-by-default directive; tracking: Plan #8 task #69; expiry/removal condition: remove only when Go adopts identical rejection or a reviewed canonical parser and migration produces byte-identical Go/Rust fixture output. | Existing automated test — TestNormalizeGitRejectsCredentialsAndRiskyRefspecs (normalize_test.go:79) | internal/capability/normalize.go:345-418 |

### SSH scope normalization

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CAP-019` | Capabilities | `AllowInteractiveShell=true` is always rejected at normalization: `interactive SSH shells are unavailable through the capability broker transport`. | Existing automated test — TestNormalizeSSHRejectsUnavailableInteractiveShellGrant (normalize_test.go:201) | internal/capability/normalize.go:502-504 |
| `CAP-020` | Capabilities | SSH host: lowercased, trailing dot stripped, brackets trimmed; IP or DNS name with per-label rules (≤63 chars, no leading/trailing `-`, letters/digits/`-` only), total ≤253; port defaults to 22; user is required and must match token charset (letters/digits/`._-/`, ≤128, no leading `-`); alias (optional) same charset without `/`. **Reviewed Rust security compatibility waiver:** affected targets are Rust SSH normalization on macOS, Linux, and Windows. Observed difference: aliases containing `/` are rejected explicitly even though the shared Go token helper currently permits them. User impact: such aliases must be renamed before preview or authorization; host/user transport identity is unchanged. Security/data-compatibility risk: fail-closed denial can make an existing grant unusable, while slash-bearing aliases can be confused with path-like identifiers in settings, diagnostics, or future lookup layers. Rationale: enforce the documented alias grammar and keep aliases non-hierarchical. Approved by the Plan #8 owner's end-to-end and deny-by-default directive; tracking: Plan #8 task #69; expiry/removal condition: remove only after Go enforces the documented grammar or a reviewed product contract deliberately defines hierarchical aliases with migration and collision tests. Host key must be exactly `<type> <base64>` with type starting `ssh-` or `ecdsa-` and valid std base64 (`SSH host key must contain exactly a key type and base64 public key`). | Existing automated test — TestNormalizeSSHSeparatesHighRiskGrants (normalize_test.go:147) | internal/capability/normalize.go:505-527,671-730 |
| `CAP-021` | Capabilities | Remote commands must be exact, non-empty, single-line, untrimmed-equal strings (no `\r`, `\n`, NUL). **Reviewed Rust security compatibility waiver:** affected targets are Rust SSH normalization on macOS, Linux, and Windows. Observed difference: Unicode U+2028/U+2029 line separators are also rejected; canonical JSON escaping remains byte-compatible for persisted Go values. User impact: commands containing those separators must be replaced by printable single-line commands and explicitly re-approved. Security/data-compatibility risk: fail-closed denial may interrupt the grant, while accepting Unicode line boundaries creates display/audit ambiguity around an exact command authority. Rationale: a remotely executed command must have one visible single-line representation. Approved by the Plan #8 owner's end-to-end and deny-by-default directive; tracking: Plan #8 task #69; expiry/removal condition: remove only if Go rejects the same separators or terminal, settings, audit, and executor layers prove and fixture one unambiguous representation end to end. | Existing automated test — TestThreatUnapprovedSSHMetacharactersNeverReachProcessRunner (threat_test.go:24) | internal/capability/normalize.go:528-533 |
| `CAP-022` | Capabilities | Upload/download grants must be paired with their root lists both ways (`AllowUpload` ⇔ non-empty `UploadRoots`+`UploadRemoteRoots`, same for download); violations: `SSH local and remote upload roots must be configured with upload approval` (and download twin). | Existing automated test — TestNormalizeSSHRejectsTraversalAndUnpairedTransferGrant (normalize_test.go:188) | internal/capability/normalize.go:534-539 |
| `CAP-023` | Capabilities | Local transfer roots must be project-relative (not absolute, no control chars, no `..` escape after Clean) and are stored slash-separated; remote roots must be absolute POSIX paths of letters/digits/`/._-` only, not `/` (`remote path must be an absolute POSIX path`, `remote path contains shell-significant characters`, `remote root cannot be the entire host filesystem`). | Existing automated test — TestNormalizeSSHRejectsTraversalAndUnpairedTransferGrant | internal/capability/normalize.go:540-563,622-653 |
| `CAP-024` | Capabilities | Forwarding targets normalize to `host:port` (port 1–65535) via `net.SplitHostPort`; both directions stored independently. | Existing automated test — TestSSHForwardingIsLoopbackAndDirectionScoped (ssh_test.go:202) | internal/capability/normalize.go:564-575,655-669 |
| `CAP-025` | Capabilities | At least one SSH grant (git, commands, upload, download, local-forward, remote-forward) is required: `SSH scope must approve at least one operation`. Effective-scope string: `alias=%q <user>@<host>:<port> host-key=SHA256:<b64> grants=<json> commands=<json> upload_local_roots=<json> upload_remote_roots=<json> download_local_roots=<json> download_remote_roots=<json> local_forward_targets=<json> remote_forward_targets=<json>`. | Existing automated test — TestNormalizeSSHEffectiveScopeDisplaysExactHighRiskTargets (normalize_test.go:167) | internal/capability/normalize.go:582-620,682-693 |

Query strings are permitted in requests but never enter persisted/audited scope. | Existing automated test — TestHTTPWriteMethodAndPathRequireExactApproval (policy_test.go:138) | internal/capability/policy.go:148-191 |

| `CAP-035` | Capabilities | `AuthorizeSSH`: kind ssh (`capability is not SSH`); each of the 7 operations (`git`, `remote-command`, `upload`, `download`, `interactive-shell`, `local-forward`, `remote-forward`) is authorized independently — no grant implies another; `remote-command` requires exact string membership, forwards require normalized endpoint membership; failures: `SSH %s is not approved`, unknown op `unknown SSH operation %q`. | Existing automated test — TestSSHGrantsDoNotCompose (policy_test.go:71) | internal/capability/policy.go:34-45,103-146 |

### HTTP executor

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CAP-063` | Capabilities | Request DTO `HTTPRequest{method, url, headers?, body?}` (body base64 in tool schema); response DTO `HTTPResponse{status_code, status, headers, body, effective_url, redirects, diagnostics{proxy, ca_store}}`; body returned raw (base64 in JSON). | Existing automated test — TestHTTPExecutorFollowsConfinedRedirectAndStripsSecrets (http_test.go:27) | internal/capability/http.go:21-48 |
| `CAP-064` | Capabilities | Header policy: `Host`, `Proxy-Authorization`, and hop-by-hop headers (`Connection, Keep-Alive, Proxy-Connection, Te, Trailer, Transfer-Encoding, Upgrade`) rejected (`HTTP header %q is not allowed`); CR/LF in names/values rejected (`HTTP header %q contains a newline`); total name+value bytes ≤ 64 KiB (`HTTP headers exceed their byte limit`). | New automated test needed | internal/capability/http.go:155-177 |
| `CAP-065` | Capabilities | Redirects: each hop re-runs full `AuthorizeHTTP` on the new method+URL before the request is sent (`redirect rejected: capability denied: ...`); hop count > `MaxRedirects` → `capability denied: HTTP redirect limit exceeded`; `Authorization`, `Cookie`, `Proxy-Authorization` are stripped on redirect. | Existing automated test — TestHTTPExecutorFollowsConfinedRedirectAndStripsSecrets, TestHTTPExecutorRejectsRedirectEscapeBeforeSecondRequest (http_test.go:51), TestHTTPExecutorRejectsFirstRedirectWhenLimitIsZero (http_test.go:67) | internal/capability/http.go:117-130,179-183 |
| `CAP-066` | Capabilities | Networking uses `http.ProxyFromEnvironment` and the system CA store; response headers capped at 64 KiB; diagnostics report sanitized proxy (`direct` / `error` / URL with userinfo+query+fragment stripped) and `ca_store: "system"`; an HTTP 407 response becomes error `HTTP proxy requires authentication`. **Reviewed Rust compatibility waiver:** affected targets are Rust HTTP execution on macOS, Linux, and Windows. Observed differences: reqwest/hyper parses response headers before the executor applies the exact 64 KiB decoded name+value bound, rather than rejecting at the socket parser at precisely 64 KiB; its system-proxy connector does not expose the selected OS proxy, so diagnostics reproduce environment/CGI/`NO_PROXY` selection and otherwise report `direct` even when an opaque OS proxy may be selected internally. User impact: a hostile peer may make the HTTP library transiently parse more header bytes before rejection, and the diagnostic can under-report an OS-configured proxy; no oversized headers, proxy credentials, URLs, or bodies are returned or audited. Security/data-compatibility risk: bounded application data, platform CA verification, proxy routing, and durable audit boundaries remain enforced, but parser allocation is controlled by the reviewed upstream library and the proxy diagnostic is advisory rather than authority. Rationale: the locked system-proxy and platform-CA client exposes neither an HTTP/1 parser byte limit nor the chosen OS proxy; replacing it with custom TLS/proxy code would materially expand cryptographic and platform risk. Approved by the Plan #8 owner's end-to-end and deny-by-default directive; tracking: Plan #8 task #69; expiry/removal condition: remove each difference when the locked client exposes the corresponding reviewed parser limit or sanitized selected-proxy API on all targets, then add raw-socket and OS-proxy fixtures. | Existing automated test — TestHTTPDiagnosticsRedactProxyCredentials (http_test.go:109); Rust `http_response_stream_and_decoded_headers_are_bounded`, `proxy_diagnostics_honor_no_proxy_and_redact_credentials` | internal/capability/http.go:105-116,149-151,185-198,212-214; crates/ptrack-capability/src/http.rs |
| `CAP-067` | Capabilities | Response body read with limit+1; overflow → error `HTTP response exceeds its byte limit`. Timeout is `Limits.TimeoutSeconds` over the whole exchange including redirects. | Existing automated test — TestHTTPExecutorEnforcesResponseLimitAndTimeout (http_test.go:86) | internal/capability/http.go:93,136-142,208-210 |
| `CAP-068` | Capabilities | `ClassifyConnectionError` mapping: ErrDenied→`denied`; response-limit type→`response-limit`; proxy 407 type→`proxy`; `context.DeadlineExceeded`→`timeout`; `context.Canceled`→`cancelled`; `*net.DNSError`→`dns`; x509 unknown-authority/invalid-cert→`tls`; ENETUNREACH/EHOSTUNREACH/ENETDOWN/ECONNREFUSED→`routing`; EPERM/os.ErrPermission→`sandbox`; `*url.Error` unwrapped recursively; else `transport`. | Existing automated test — TestClassifyConnectionError (http_test.go:128) | internal/capability/http.go:216-262 |

### Git executor

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CAP-069` | Capabilities | Before any operation the executor re-verifies repository identity with fresh subprocesses: `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` must canonicalize to the project root (`Git repository identity could not be verified` / `Git repository root does not match the project`); `git config --get-all remote.<name>.url` must yield exactly one untrimmed-equal URL (`Git remote could not be verified` / `Git remote is invalid`); any `remote.<name>.{pushurl,uploadpack,receivepack}` override → `Git remote overrides make the approved operation ambiguous`; any `url.*.{insteadOf,pushInsteadOf}` → `Git URL rewrite rules make the approved remote ambiguous` (exit code 1 from `--get-regexp` means "no match"; other errors deny). | Existing automated test — TestGitExecutorDeniesChangedRemoteAndURLRewritesBeforeOperation (git_test.go:96), TestGitExecutorRejectsMultipleURLsAndRemoteCommandOverrides (git_test.go:120), TestGitExecutorRejectsSymlinkedProjectIdentity (git_test.go:176) | internal/capability/git.go:61-116 |
| `CAP-070` | Capabilities | All git invocations are direct `exec` of `git -C <root>` with env `LC_ALL=C, LANG=C, GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0, GCM_INTERACTIVE=Never` appended to the host environment; no shell. **Reviewed Rust security compatibility waiver:** affected targets are Rust Git capability execution on macOS, Linux, and Windows. Observed difference: inherited variables whose case-folded names begin `GIT_` are removed before the fixed variables are installed; Rust also fixes `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` and `core.fsmonitor=false`. User impact: callers cannot inject alternate repositories, config, object stores, SSH commands, lazy-fetch behavior, or fsmonitor helpers through the executor environment; partial-clone operations requiring an implicit lazy fetch fail and repository fsmonitor acceleration is bypassed. Security/data-compatibility risk: fail-closed operations may need an explicit ordinary fetch, while retaining ambient Git authority could bypass the approved repository, transport, and no-code-execution boundary. Rationale: direct argv alone is insufficient while Git treats environment and repository fsmonitor configuration as executable authority. Approved by the Plan #8 owner's end-to-end and deny-by-default directive; tracking: Plan #8 task #69; expiry/removal condition: none for the deny-by-default runtime; revisit only if the Go contract adopts equivalent scrubbing and hardening. | Existing automated test — TestGitExecutorUsesFreshExactRemoteAndFixedFetchArgs (git_test.go:55); Rust `process_environment_scrubs_all_ambient_git_authority`, `git_fetch_uses_fresh_identity_fixed_args_and_random_alias` | internal/capability/git.go:126,190-196, internal/capability/process.go:38-58; crates/ptrack-capability/src/process.rs, crates/ptrack-capability/src/git.rs |
| `CAP-071` | Capabilities | Fixed config flags on every operation: `-c core.hooksPath=<fresh empty temp dir>`, `protocol.allow=never`, `protocol.ext.allow=never`, `submodule.recurse=false`, `fetch.recurseSubmodules=false`, `push.recurseSubmodules=no`, plus `protocol.ssh.allow=always` or `protocol.https.allow=always` matching the approved scheme. | Existing automated test — TestGitExecutorUsesFreshExactRemoteAndFixedFetchArgs | internal/capability/git.go:148-152,205-219 |
| `CAP-072` | Capabilities | The mutable remote name is never used on the command line: a random alias `ptrack-approved-<48 hex>://remote` is generated per call and resolved once via command-scope `-c url.<approvedURL>.insteadOf=<alias>` and `.pushInsteadOf=<alias>`. | Existing automated test — TestGitExecutorPinsPushToExactApprovedHeadAndURL (git_test.go:78) | internal/capability/git.go:220-228,273-279 |
| `CAP-073` | Capabilities | Fixed argv per operation: `status --short --branch`; `fetch --no-recurse-submodules (--tags\|--no-tags) -- <alias> [<refspec>\|refs/heads/<branch>]`; `pull --ff-only --no-rebase --no-recurse-submodules [--no-tags] -- <alias> refs/heads/<branch>`; `push [--force-with-lease] -- <alias> [<refspec>\|refs/heads/<b>:refs/heads/<b>]`; `ls-remote -- <alias> [refs/heads/<branch>]`. Any other operation → `capability denied: unsupported Git operation`. | Existing automated test — TestGitExecutorUsesFreshExactRemoteAndFixedFetchArgs, TestGitExecutorPinsPushToExactApprovedHeadAndURL | internal/capability/git.go:229-269 |
| `CAP-074` | Capabilities | Git-over-SSH requires a separate SSH capability whose `AllowGit` grant authorizes and whose normalized `user@host:port` exactly matches the remote (`Git-over-SSH requires a separate SSH grant`, `Git remote does not match the approved SSH host identity`); transport is `GIT_SSH_VARIANT=ssh` + `GIT_SSH_COMMAND=ssh <sshBaseArgs with pinned known_hosts>` (shell-quoted single string). SCP-style remotes must include a user; `ssh://` remotes must include a user; default port 22. | Existing automated test — TestGitOverSSHRequiresMatchingPinnedSSHGrant (git_test.go:152) | internal/capability/git.go:128-145,281-328 |
| `CAP-075` | Capabilities | `GitResult{exit_code, stdout?, stderr?, diagnostic}`; truncated output → `diagnostic: "output-limit"` + error `process output exceeds its byte limit`; non-zero/failed run → `diagnostic` = class + error `Git operation failed: <class>`; success → `diagnostic: "none"`. Stdout/stderr share one aggregate byte budget (see CAP-088). | Existing automated test — git_test.go | internal/capability/git.go:28-34,176-187,330-332 |
| `CAP-076` | Capabilities | `ClassifyGitError`: denied→`denied`; output limit→`output-limit`; deadline→`timeout`; canceled→`cancelled`; then stderr (lowercased) substring rules: "could not resolve host(name)"→`dns`; "ssl certificate problem"/"certificate verify failed"→`tls`; "host key verification failed"→`host-key`; "authentication failed"/"could not read username"/"permission denied (publickey"→`authentication`; "protected branch"/"remote rejected"/"repository not found"/"permission to"→`remote-policy`; "connection refused"/"no route to host"/"failed to connect"→`routing`; else falls back to `ClassifyConnectionError`. Raw stderr is never persisted. | Existing automated test — TestClassifyGitError (git_test.go:194) | internal/capability/git.go:334-370 |

| `HTTP-007` | Agent integration HTTP | `/v1/runs/{id}/events`: lease authenticated BEFORE body decode; on success → 201 `{id, hostSequence, observedAt}` receipt; lease errors → 401 `AgentRun lease rejected`; validation errors → 400 `AgentRun event rejected` | Existing automated test — integration_test.go TestIntegrationServerRegisterHeartbeatExitAndCleanup | internal/agentrun/integration.go:265-285,331-343 |
| `HTTP-008` | Agent integration HTTP | `POST /v1/events` (launched agents, run-ID-free): Bearer launched-event token awaited up to 2s for host binding; pending/unknown/timeout → 401 `AgentRun event token rejected`; then ProviderEvent decoded and recorded via bound run; same 201 receipt; validation errors → 400 `AgentRun event rejected` | Existing automated test — integration_test.go TestIntegrationServerBindsLaunchedEventTokenToOwnedRun, event_registry_test.go TestPendingLaunchedTokenWaitsForHostBinding | internal/agentrun/integration.go:23,293-323 |

## Git intelligence, reporting, and guide

51 contracts. The table is both the compatibility specification and the parity acceptance matrix for this subsystem.

### GIT — bounded subprocess execution (runner.go)

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GIT-001` | Git intelligence | Every git invocation is `git --no-optional-locks -C <root> <args...>`; the root is always injected, never the process cwd. **Reviewed Rust security compatibility deviation:** the Rust port permanently inserts `-c core.fsmonitor=false` before `-C`, preventing repository-controlled fsmonitor hook execution during read-only inspection. User impact: a configured fsmonitor is bypassed for these bounded reads only; Git results remain the contract. Risk/rationale: accepting repository code execution would violate the deny-by-default boundary. Approved by the Plan #8 owner's end-to-end directive; tracking issue: Plan #8 task #67; expiry/removal condition: none (permanent security tightening). | Existing automated test — TestGitCommandArgsAreReadOnlyAndRootScoped (runner_test.go); Rust `git_command_is_hardened_and_root_scoped`, `malicious_repository_fsmonitor_is_never_executed` | internal/gitinfo/runner.go:85-90; crates/ptrack-git/src/runner.rs |
| `GIT-002` | Git intelligence | Child environment sanitization: every inherited key whose uppercase form starts with `GIT_` is dropped (defeats `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE`/`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` injection); `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS`, `GIT_PAGER`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT` are then force-set to `C`, `C`, `0`, `cat`, `0` respectively (case-insensitive match against inherited keys); all other inherited vars pass through; the five overrides are appended in that fixed order. **Reviewed Rust security compatibility deviation:** the Rust port appends `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` as a sixth fixed override, preventing read-only inspection from contacting a promisor remote. User impact: missing partial-clone objects fail instead of being fetched; no ordinary local-repository output changes. Risk/rationale: ambient network access would violate the deny-by-default capability boundary. Approved by the Plan #8 owner's end-to-end directive; tracking issue: Plan #8 task #67; expiry/removal condition: none (permanent security tightening). | Existing automated test — TestGitEnvironmentDisablesWritesPagerAndPrompts; Rust `git_environment_scrubs_case_insensitively_and_appends_fixed_values` | internal/gitinfo/runner.go:92-130; crates/ptrack-git/src/runner.rs |
| `GIT-003` | Git intelligence | Per-command bounds: default timeout 3s (`defaultCommandTimeout`), default combined-output cap 4 MiB (`defaultOutputLimit`); zero/negative `Timeout`/`MaxOutputBytes` fall back to defaults; empty `GitPath` falls back to `"git"` (PATH lookup) | New automated test needed | internal/gitinfo/runner.go:15-18,45-60 |
| `GIT-004` | Git intelligence | Error taxonomy and precedence: caller-context error returned first (ctx.Err()), then `ErrCommandTimeout` ("git command timed out", deadline on the derived context), then `ErrOutputLimit` ("git command output limit exceeded"), then `ErrCommandFailed` ("git command failed") wrapping the exit error as `git command failed: %v` | Existing automated test — TestExecRunnerBoundsOutput, TestExecRunnerHonorsCancellationAndTimeout | internal/gitinfo/runner.go:20-24,70-81 |
| `GIT-005` | Git intelligence | Stdout and stderr share one combined byte budget; once the cap is hit further bytes are consumed and discarded (child never blocks, memory stays bounded) and only the truncated stdout prefix would have been returned — but any exceedance yields ErrOutputLimit with nil output | Existing automated test — TestExecRunnerBoundsOutput | internal/gitinfo/runner.go:132-186 |
| `GIT-006` | Git intelligence | Only stdout is returned to callers; stderr is buffered (for the cap) but never surfaced, even on failure | New automated test needed | internal/gitinfo/runner.go:67-82 |

Timeout/output-limit/cancellation errors propagate immediately without the marker check | Existing automated test — TestCaptureReportsNonRepositoryWithoutError, TestCapturePropagatesRepositoryIdentityFailureWhenGitMarkerExists, TestCapturePropagatesCancellationAndResourceLimits | internal/gitinfo/snapshot.go:144-155,302-321 |

But `status`, both `for-each-ref` calls, and the divergence `rev-list` are fatal on any error. Non-ErrCommandFailed errors (timeout/limit/cancel) are fatal everywhere except they never reach the tolerant branches | Existing automated test (partial) — partially (TestCaptureBuildsBoundedReadOnlyGitSnapshot happy path; failure paths unpinned)<br>New automated test needed | internal/gitinfo/snapshot.go:160-171,188-201,210-236,240-258,260-297 |
| `GIT-013` | Git intelligence | Status probe: `git status --porcelain=v2 --branch -z --ignored=matching` | Existing automated test — TestCaptureAgainstRealRepository | internal/gitinfo/snapshot.go:173-186 |
| `GIT-014` | Git intelligence | Remotes probe: `git config --null --get-regexp '^remote\..*\.(url\|pushurl)$'`; NUL-separated `key\nvalue` entries; keys must end `.url` or `.pushurl` ("unexpected Git remote config key", "empty Git remote name", "malformed Git remote config" errors otherwise); remotes sorted by name, capped at 16 (`maxRemotes`); when a remote has no pushurl, `pushUrls` falls back to a copy of `fetchUrls` | Existing automated test — TestCaptureBuildsBoundedReadOnlyGitSnapshot | internal/gitinfo/snapshot.go:188-201,348-409 |
| `GIT-015` | Git intelligence | Branch probes: two `git for-each-ref --count=N --sort=-committerdate --format=%(refname)%00%(objectname)%00%(upstream:short)%00%(committerdate:unix)%00%(HEAD)%00%(worktreepath)%00` calls — N=100 for `refs/heads`, N=150 for `refs/remotes`; six NUL-separated fields per record; `lastCommitAt` = unix epoch formatted RFC3339 in UTC; `current` = HEAD field `*`; local `stale` = non-current branch whose tip commit is older than 90 days (`staleBranchAge`) relative to capture time; remote-branch entries ending `/HEAD` are dropped; a ref in the wrong namespace aborts Capture with "local Git ref query returned remote refs" / "remote Git ref query returned local refs"; other namespaces are parse errors ("unexpected Git ref namespace") | Existing automated test — TestCaptureBuildsBoundedReadOnlyGitSnapshot, TestParseRefsAndLogsRejectMalformedRecords | internal/gitinfo/snapshot.go:19-25,203-238,411-457 |
