## Updater

26 contracts. The table is both the compatibility specification and the parity acceptance matrix for this subsystem.

Rust parity evidence for this inventory is split across sibling-only tests:
`crates/ptrack-updater/src/discovery_test.rs` (UPD-001–006),
`staging_test.rs` (UPD-007–015), `installer_test.rs` (UPD-016–022),
and `crates/ptrack-app/src/update_runtime_test.rs` plus
`desktop_runtime_test.rs` (UPD-006, UPD-023–024). The Windows private-path
module is additionally MSVC type-checked; native handoff/security execution and
the deliberately opt-in live GitHub checks remain acceptance evidence under
task #74 and are not inferred from cross-builds (UPD-007, UPD-017–026).

### Discovery and candidate selection

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UPD-001` | Updater | Release discovery uses only `https://api.github.com/repos/ro-ag/ptrack/releases/latest`, sends the fixed updater headers, refuses metadata redirects, times out after 15 seconds, and reads at most 1 MiB of metadata. | Existing automated test — internal/updater/discovery_test.go/TestCheckBoundsAndCancelsMetadataRequest, TestCheckSendsBoundedGitHubRequestHeaders, TestCheckRejectsMetadataRedirectAndStatus, TestNewClientHasProductionSafetyDefaults | internal/updater/discovery.go:17-23,66-75,142-183 |
| `UPD-002` | Updater | The running version must be strict stable `X.Y.Z` with an optional leading `v`; malformed, prerelease, metadata, whitespace, or leading-zero versions are development builds and cannot update. Version comparison is numeric over exactly three uint64 components. | Existing automated test — internal/updater/discovery_test.go/TestParseVersionStrictness, TestCompareVersions, TestCheckRejectsDevelopmentUnsupportedAndNonNewerVersions | internal/updater/discovery.go:65-72,238-298 |
| `UPD-003` | Updater | A candidate must be a published, non-draft, non-prerelease release whose tag is canonical `vX.Y.Z`, whose version is strictly newer, whose publication time is non-zero, and whose release notes are at most 32 KiB. | Existing automated test (partial) — internal/updater/discovery_test.go/TestCheckSelectsExactPackagedAsset, TestCheckRejectsDevelopmentUnsupportedAndNonNewerVersions, TestCheckRejectsUnsafeReleaseMetadata (release selection only; 32-KiB notes boundary unpinned)<br>New automated test needed | internal/updater/discovery.go:76-103 |
| `UPD-004` | Updater | Supported update targets are exactly darwin, linux, and windows on amd64 or arm64. Exact package names are `p-track_<version>_darwin_<arch>.dmg`, `ptrack_<version>_linux_<arch>.tar.gz`, and `ptrack_<version>_windows_<arch>.zip`. | Existing automated test — internal/updater/discovery_test.go/TestCheckSelectsExactPackagedAsset, TestCheckRejectsDevelopmentUnsupportedAndNonNewerVersions | internal/updater/discovery.go:186-200 |
| `UPD-005` | Updater | Discovery requires exactly one expected package and one `checksums.txt`; each must be in state `uploaded`, have a positive bounded size (package ≤512 MiB, manifest ≤1 MiB), and use an exact query-free, fragment-free HTTPS GitHub release-download URL for the selected tag and filename. Missing, duplicate, arbitrary, or source assets never become candidates. | Existing automated test — internal/updater/discovery_test.go/TestCheckRejectsUnsafeReleaseMetadata, TestCheckIgnoresGitHubSourceArchiveFields | internal/updater/discovery.go:17-23,94-112,202-236 |
| `UPD-006` | Updater | The candidate exposed to the GUI is reduced to version, UTC publication time, bounded notes, fixed release page, and package size; asset URLs and local stage paths never cross the frontend boundary. | Existing automated test — internal/gui/update_test.go/TestUpdateCheckWorksWithoutProjectAndExposesNoAssetAuthority | internal/updater/discovery.go:48-56; internal/gui/update.go:689-696 |

### Download and private staging

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UPD-007` | Updater | Stages live below the absolute updater directory as private `.stage-*` directories. Unix directories/files are 0700/0600, symlinks are rejected, and files reopen with `O_NOFOLLOW`; Windows uses protected current-user-only DACLs and rejects reparse points. | Existing automated test (partial) — internal/updater/staging_test.go/TestStageVerifiesAndValidatesEveryPlatformPayload (Unix root mode only; file modes, O_NOFOLLOW, Windows DACLs, and reparse rejection unpinned)<br>New automated test needed<br>Manual acceptance `MANUAL-UPD-007` | internal/updater/staging.go:320-335; internal/updater/permissions_unix.go:13-67; internal/updater/permissions_windows.go:14-128 |
| `UPD-008` | Updater | Asset downloads time out after 10 minutes and follow at most two redirects. Redirects remove Authorization and Cookie, stay on HTTPS GitHub asset hosts (`release-assets.githubusercontent.com` or `objects.githubusercontent.com`), reject userinfo, fragments, explicit ports, empty paths, and any other host. | Existing automated test (partial) — internal/updater/staging_test.go/TestStageRejectsChecksumAndDownloadFailuresAndCleansPartialState (evil-host redirect only; redirect count, header stripping, userinfo, fragments, ports, and empty paths unpinned)<br>New automated test needed | internal/updater/staging.go:31,338-471 |
| `UPD-009` | Updater | Each download must return HTTP 200 and match both release-metadata Content-Length when present and the exact advertised byte count. It is written with create-exclusive semantics, SHA-256 hashed while streaming, fsynced, closed, and secured before publication; partial files and failed stage roots are removed. | Existing automated test — internal/updater/staging_test.go/TestStageRejectsChecksumAndDownloadFailuresAndCleansPartialState | internal/updater/staging.go:117-126,338-427 |

### Checksum and archive validation

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UPD-010` | Updater | `checksums.txt` accepts at most 256 lines. Its scanner declares a 512-byte maximum but supplies a 1024-byte initial buffer, so Go accepts over-512-byte lines that fit the initial buffer and rejects only when token growth is required; parity must preserve this effective boundary or waive a deliberate hardening change. Every line has a 64-hex SHA-256 followed by a safe base filename; the selected package must have exactly one entry. Missing, duplicate, malformed, path-bearing, or mismatching entries fail closed. | Existing automated test (partial) — internal/updater/staging_test.go/TestStageRejectsChecksumAndDownloadFailuresAndCleansPartialState (malformed, missing, duplicate, and mismatch cases only; line count, effective line size, and path-bearing names unpinned)<br>New automated test needed | internal/updater/staging.go:29-30,474-514 |
| `UPD-011` | Updater | Linux tar.gz and Windows ZIP packages contain exactly `ptrack`/`ptrack.exe`, `README.md`, and `LICENSE` at the archive root. Entries are unique regular files; links, directories beyond the optional tar root, encryption, traversal, extra entries, and duplicates are rejected. Each entry is ≤128 MiB and total expanded content ≤160 MiB. | Existing automated test (partial) — internal/updater/staging_test.go/TestStageRejectsUnsafeArchiveLayoutsAndMachines (unsafe layouts and machines only; duplicate entries, encryption, extra directories, and size limits unpinned)<br>New automated test needed | internal/updater/staging.go:517-625 |
| `UPD-012` | Updater | The Linux payload must be a 64-bit little-endian executable ELF for x86-64 or AArch64; the Windows payload must be an executable PE for AMD64 or ARM64. macOS retains and later verifies the complete DMG rather than extracting an executable. | Existing automated test — internal/updater/staging_test.go/TestStageRejectsUnsafeArchiveLayoutsAndMachines | internal/updater/staging.go:657-723 |

### Platform handoff

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UPD-016` | Updater | Installer apply requires the staged GOOS/GOARCH to equal the running host, revalidates the complete stage, and invokes fixed platform command shapes with trusted host-derived paths; Linux executes the host-derived target path with `version`. | `UPD-017` | Updater | macOS apply accepts only the whole DMG, reruns `hdiutil verify`, `codesign --verify --strict` with pinned Team ID `3CAJR4ZDMQ`, and Gatekeeper `spctl`; only then does `/usr/bin/open` hand off the DMG. The installed app is never modified in place by p-track. | Existing automated test — internal/updater/apply_darwin_test.go/TestDarwinApplyVerifiesTrustBeforeOpeningDMG, TestDarwinApplyStopsBeforeOpenWhenVerificationFails<br>Manual acceptance `MANUAL-UPD-017` | internal/updater/apply_darwin.go:10-33; docs/updater-acceptance.md |

| `UPD-019` | Updater | Linux apply accepts only a Linux-binary stage, resolves the running executable through symlinks, opens it without following links, requires current-user ownership with no setuid/setgid, requires a current-user-owned non-group/world-writable parent, requires an executable non-group/world-writable mode, and holds a nonblocking target-keyed flock. | Existing automated test — internal/updater/apply_linux_test.go/TestLinuxApplyRefusesUnsafeOwnershipAndModes, TestLinuxApplyLockIsTargetScopedAndExclusive<br>Manual acceptance `MANUAL-UPD-019` | internal/updater/apply_linux.go:21-105,230-287,371-407 |
| `UPD-020` | Updater | Linux replacement copies and rehashes the verified payload into a sibling temporary file, preserves the safe executable mode, fsyncs it, confirms the original inode is unchanged, creates and verifies a hard-link backup plus durable journal, atomically renames, syncs the directory, and requires `strings.TrimSpace` of the new binary's `version` output to equal `ptrack <version>`; leading/trailing whitespace is therefore accepted. Any failure rolls back. | Existing automated test — internal/updater/apply_linux_test.go/TestLinuxApplyAtomicallyReplacesAndVerifiesUserOwnedBinary, TestLinuxApplyRollsBackFailedReplacementVerification<br>Manual acceptance `MANUAL-UPD-020` | internal/updater/apply_linux.go:44-139 |
| `UPD-021` | Updater | A successful Linux install reports restart required. Failure to remove the verified backup, sync the directory, or remove the journal does not undo an already verified install; it returns success with `cleanupPending:true` for recovery attention. | New automated test needed | internal/updater/apply_linux.go:122-139 |
| `UPD-022` | Updater | The Linux pending-apply journal is private JSON binding version, stage root, target, sibling backup, original device/inode, and payload digest/size. Recovery removes state only when the target is provably the unchanged original or the verified replacement; mismatched, unsafe, or ambiguous state is preserved for manual recovery. | Existing automated test — internal/updater/apply_linux_test.go/TestRecoverPendingLinuxApplyCleansVerifiedSwap, TestRecoverPendingLinuxApplyRejectsTargetAsBackup, TestRecoverPendingLinuxApplyIdentifiesAnotherVerifiedStage<br>Manual acceptance `MANUAL-UPD-022` | internal/updater/apply_linux.go:140-221,289-369 |

### Consent, recovery, and trust boundary

| ID | Subsystem | Checkable contract | Verification method | Go freeze source / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UPD-023` | Updater | Update checks are manual unless the user has persisted an opt-in; check, download, and install are separate single-flight actions. Opt-out or cancel signals the active context but retains the operation fence until the worker exits. Public errors and frontend state are bounded and omit transport details, asset URLs, stage paths, and credentials. | Existing automated test — internal/gui/update_test.go/TestCanceledUpdateKeepsSingleFlightFenceUntilWorkerExits, TestUpdateErrorsAreBoundedAndDoNotExposeTransportDetails<br>Manual acceptance `MANUAL-UPD-023` | internal/gui/update.go:90-153,155-348,420-560; docs/updater-security.md |
| `UPD-024` | Updater | Startup recovery scans at most 64 name-sorted `.stage-*` directories below the updater home. Unreadable, invalid, or wrong-host stages are skipped; more than 64 stage directories, an unresolved recover error, or any remaining Linux `.pending-apply-*.json` blocks new update authority. Otherwise the newest valid same-host stage newer than the running version becomes ready and other valid stages are discarded. Non-Linux pending-apply recovery is a no-op because those platforms use manual native handoff. | Existing automated test (partial) — internal/gui/update_test.go/TestStartupRecoveryFailsClosedBeforeManualChecks (>64 backlog only; skipped stages, unresolved recovery, remaining journals, newest selection, and superseded disposal unpinned)<br>New automated test needed | internal/gui/update.go:350-396,562-669; internal/updater/recover_other.go:1-8 |
| `UPD-025` | Updater | The package SHA-256 and `checksums.txt` are co-hosted in the same GitHub Release. They detect corruption and mismatched assets but are not an independent publisher signature if release authority is compromised. macOS adds pinned Developer ID and Gatekeeper verification; Windows and Linux rely on the release account plus the co-hosted checksum. | Manual acceptance `MANUAL-UPD-025` / security review | docs/updater-security.md; internal/updater/staging.go; internal/updater/apply_darwin.go:10-28 |
| `UPD-026` | Updater | Updater acceptance requires the documented automated suite, native OS-tagged tests, and interactive handoff on exact OS/architecture hosts. Cross-compilation is evidence for buildability but does not count as native execution; live GitHub release and macOS trust tests run only with `PTRACK_LIVE_UPDATE_TEST=1`. | Existing automated test — TestLiveLatestReleaseContract, TestLiveDarwinDMGTrustContract<br>Manual acceptance `MANUAL-UPD-026` | docs/updater-acceptance.md; internal/updater/staging_test.go:25-44; internal/updater/apply_darwin_test.go |

## Acceptance gate definition

Plan 8 has achieved parity with the `v0.21.0` Go freeze only when all of the
following are true. Full current-feature parity additionally requires the
post-tag extension described in the scope section; this gate must not be
interpreted as waiving those later features.

1. Every row in this matrix has a result against the exact Rust candidate
   revision. A result is `pass`, `not-applicable`, or `waived`; missing,
   `unavailable`, and `skipped` are not passing results.
2. The release targets are the six native workflow targets:
   `darwin/amd64`, `darwin/arm64`, `linux/amd64`, `linux/arm64`,
   `windows/amd64`, and `windows/arm64`. Every row is verified on every
   applicable target. A platform-specific row may be `not-applicable` only
   with the inapplicable targets named and the code/build boundary cited.
3. Portable byte and protocol contracts use checked-in golden fixtures
   produced by Go tag commit `b7727c5`, plus Rust read/write or
   request/response tests. Existing project databases must either open and
   round-trip compatibly or pass the explicit migration contract; silent
   format replacement is not parity.
4. All rows marked **New automated test needed** have a test before the gate
   closes. All named automated suites, Rust tests, formatters, linters, and
   frontend tests pass. OS-tagged behavior executes natively; cross-compile
   success alone is insufficient.
5. TUI, GUI, terminal, accessibility, updater, signing, installation, and
   recovery procedures have current native result records. Use
   `docs/terminal-acceptance.md` and `docs/updater-acceptance.md` where they
   apply. Do not record terminal contents, credentials, tokens, URLs with
   authority, or private paths.
6. A waiver is explicit and reviewable: contract ID, affected target(s),
   observed difference, user impact, security/data-compatibility risk,
   rationale, approving owner, tracking issue, and expiry or removal
   condition. A product change is not a waiver merely because the new
   behavior is preferred. Security, stored-data readability, and release
   trust-boundary rows require an explicit design decision and migration or
   user-safety plan.
7. There are no unresolved mismatches, flaky results, hidden platform
   reductions, or unreviewed fixture changes. The final parity report links
   each row to its automated run, fixture, manual result record, or waiver.

Evidence records should minimally contain the contract ID, Rust commit,
target OS/architecture, verification method, result, date, and test/fixture
or issue reference. The gate applies to externally observable behavior, not
to matching the Go implementation structure.
